### 1. user (2026-07-02T06:11:33.378Z)

In C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer: config.json's `stitching.begin_padding` / `stitching.end_padding` are silently ignored by the live highlight compiler. `backend/main.py` (around lines 508 and 633) constructs `VideoCompiler(output_dir, watermark=...)` WITHOUT passing the configured paddings, so the live stitcher always uses the constructor defaults in `backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py:10` (begin=0.5, end=1.0). The config values are only read by `backend/pipeline/segmenters/gemini.py` (~lines 406-408) for a log-line duration estimate. This was audit finding (d) from the 2026-06-10 deep audit and was re-verified as still live on 2026-06-12. Fix: thread the typed config stitching paddings into both VideoCompiler construction sites, add a regression test asserting a config padding override changes the compiled clip boundaries. Note a cross-repo coupling: sports-data-collector `src/parsers/cvat/rally_cvat.py` detect_issues docstring (~lines 288-294) justifies not flagging vendor boundary slack by citing the compiler's 0.5/1.0 padding — if defaults/wiring change, update that rationale comment too. Follow repo conventions: branch off master, one PR, run `python run_all_tests.py`.

9	class VideoCompiler:
10	    def __init__(self, output_dir: str, end_padding: float = 1.0, begin_padding: float = 0.5,
11	                 watermark: Optional[Any] = None):
12	        self.output_dir = output_dir
13	        self.end_padding = end_padding
14	        self.begin_padding = begin_padding
15	        # Optional branding overlay. Accepts a WatermarkConfig model or a plain dict;
16	        # normalized to a dict (or None) so this module stays config-package-agnostic.
17	        self.watermark = self._normalize_watermark(watermark)
18	        os.makedirs(output_dir, exist_ok=True)
19	
20	    @staticmethod
21	    def _normalize_watermark(watermark: Optional[Any]) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
22	        if watermark is None:
23	            return None
24	        if hasattr(watermark, "model_dump"):
25	            return watermark.model_dump()
26	        if isinstance(watermark, dict):
27	            return dict(watermark)
28	        return None
29	
30	    @staticmethod
31	    def _ff_quote(path: str) -> str:
32	        """Make a filesystem path safe to embed inside single quotes in an ffmpeg
33	        filtergraph: forward slashes (Windows-friendly) + escaped single quotes."""
34	        return path.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'")
35	
36	    @staticmethod
37	    def _parse_time(val: Union[int, float, str]) -> float:
38	        """Normalizes a timestamp value to float seconds.
39	        Handles: float/int (pass-through), plain numeric strings ("12.4"),
40	        and colon-separated strings ("MM:SS", "HH:MM:SS", "H:MM:SS.ms").
41	        This mirrors the parse_time logic used in gemini.py to ensure
42	        the stitcher can always consume whatever format the indexer produced."""
43	        if isinstance(val, (int, float)):
44	            return float(val)
45	        if isinstance(val, str):
46	            val = val.strip()
47	            if ":" in val:
48	                # Handle MM:SS, HH:MM:SS, etc.
49	                parts = val.split(":")
50	                parts.reverse()
51	                total = 0.0
52	                for i, p in enumerate(parts):
53	                    try:
54	                        total += float(p) * (60 ** i)
55	                    except ValueError:
56	                        pass
57	                return total
58	            try:
59	                return float(val)
60	            except ValueError:
61	                logger.warning(f"Could not parse timestamp value '{val}' as a number. Defaulting to 0.0.")
62	                return 0.0
63	        logger.warning(f"Unexpected timestamp type {type(val).__name__} for value '{val}'. Defaulting to 0.0.")
64	        return 0.0
65	
66	    def _get_video_duration(self, video_path: str) -> float:
67	        """Probes the video file to get its total duration in seconds.
68	        Returns 0.0 if it cannot be determined."""
69	        try:
70	            result = subprocess.run(
71	                [
72	                    "ffprobe", "-v", "error",
73	                    "-show_entries", "format=duration",
74	                    "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1",
75	                    video_path
76	                ],
77	                capture_output=True, text=True, check=True
78	            )
79	            return float(result.stdout.strip())
80	        except Exception as e:
81	            logger.warning(f"Could not determine video duration via ffprobe: {e}")
82	            return 0.0

293	    backend/stitcher/compiler.py: begin_padding=0.5s, end_padding=1.0s), which
294	    absorbs the sampling uncertainty when highlights are trimmed.

297	    allowed_out = allowed_outcomes if allowed_outcomes is not None else DEFAULT_ALLOWED_OUTCOMES
298	    issues: List[Issue] = []
299	
300	    ordered = sorted(records, key=lambda r: r.rally_number)
301	    for i, r in enumerate(ordered):

303	            issues.append(Issue(
304	                "blocker", "zero_duration",
305	                f"Rally {r.rally_number}: end <= start "

38	    with patch.object(subprocess, "run", side_effect=fake_run):

96	- `@backend/config/models.py` — Pydantic v2 models; **single source of truth for defaults**.
97	  - `::AppConfig` — root. Scalars `sport='badminton'`, `ai_provider='gemini'`, `ai_model='gemini-2.5-flash'`; sections `validation`,`indexing`,`output`,`stitching` (`default_factory`). `model_config = {extra:'allow', validate_assignment:True}`. ⚠ `extra='allow'` -> unknown/typo'd config.json keys silently kept (legacy tolerance).
98	  - ⎇ `::AppConfig.get` — legacy dict-compat shim: returns nested **sections as plain dicts** (`model_dump(mode='json')`, NOT the model) so chained `config.get("indexing",{}).get(...)` keeps working; falls back to searching a leaf key in any section (sections discovered dynamically from `model_fields`). ⚠ "bit every validator on real runs" per docstring — returning the model breaks `.get`. `::AppConfig.__getitem__` raises `KeyError` for unknown top-level keys else delegates to `.get`.
99	  - `::IndexingConfig` — segmenter/detector knobs (`motion_threshold=0.08`, `min_segment_duration=3.0`, `dead_time_merge_gap=2.0`, `chunk_duration=90.0`, `chunk_overlap=10.0`, `yolo_velocity_threshold=0.15`, `yolo_frame_skip=3`, `ai_handoff_padding=2.0`, `ai_max_workers=5`, `log/store_yolo_candidates=True`, nested `tracknet`). ⚠ `default_segmenter='yolo_hybrid'` HERE but config.json overrides to `'gemini'`. ⎇ `::IndexingConfig.segmenter_must_be_registered` `@field_validator` is a **no-op pass-through** — a typo'd segmenter validates fine, only fails later at `segmenter_registry.get(...)`.
100	  - `::ValidationConfig` (`min_resolution_width=1280`,`min_resolution_height=720`,`min_fps=29.9`,`min_blur_threshold=20.0`,`max_scene_cuts_per_minute=0.5`, nested `relevance`); `::ValidationRelevanceConfig` (court HSV gates, `court_pixels_min_ratio=0.05`). `::TrackNetConfig` (WSL invocation contract: `wsl_distro='Ubuntu'`, `repo_dir`, `conda_env='TrackNetV4'`, `weights_path=''`, `queue_length=5`, `velocity_threshold=0.02`). `::OutputConfig` (`default_highlights_name`, `db_name='sports_indexer.db'`, `output_dir='output'` — CLI `--output-dir` overrides it on the typed model in `main()`). `::StitchingConfig` (`begin_padding=0.5`,`end_padding=0.5`,`use_cache=False`,`min_shot_count=1`). `::Sport` Literal of 5 sports. `::Config = AppConfig` alias.
101	- `@backend/config/loader.py` — load/save with defaults-merge.
102	  - `::DEFAULT_CONFIG_PATH = Path("config.json")` (⚠ CWD-relative, NOT repo-root). `::_get_builtin_defaults` = `AppConfig().model_dump(...)`.
103	  - `::load_config` — precedence built-in-defaults -> file overrides. ⚠ **shallow top-level merge** (`{**defaults, **file_data}`): a section in config.json REPLACES the whole defaults dict for that section (Pydantic then re-applies field defaults for missing leaves). ### 64. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.383Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Open the indexer pull request
command: cd C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer && gh pr create --title "fix(api): thread config stitching paddings into live VideoCompiler sites" --body @'
## What

`config.json stitching.{begin_padding,end_padding}` were **silently ignored** by the live highlight compiler. Both `VideoCompiler` construction sites in `backend/main.py` (`/api/compile` and the CLI `--trim` path) passed only `watermark=`, so reels always used the constructor defaults (begin 0.5 / **end 1.0**) instead of the configured values (0.5/0.5). The config values were only read by `gemini.py` for a log-line duration estimate. This is **finding (d) of the 2026-06-10 deep audit**, re-verified live on 2026-06-12.

## Changes

- **`backend/main.py`** — both construction sites now thread `begin_padding`/`end_padding` from the typed `StitchingConfig` (the API site falls back to `StitchingConfig()` when `global_config` is unset, matching the existing `getattr` defensive idiom; the CLI trim site loads the typed config it already loaded for the watermark).
- **`backend/config/__init__.py`** — re-export `StitchingConfig`.
- **`tests/test_api_endpoints.py`** — regression test drives the **real** `VideoCompiler` through `/api/compile` with mocked ffmpeg/ffprobe and asserts a config padding override (2.0/3.0) reaches the trim boundaries: `-ss 8.0 / -to 17.0` for a 10–14s segment (the unwired defaults would produce 9.5/15.0).
- **`docs/CODE_MAP.md`** — truth-sync: stitcher entry now distinguishes ctor defaults (0.5/1.0) from the config-driven live call sites (0.5/0.5); padding recipe row points at the `backend/main.py` wiring.

## Behavior change

Live API reels previously got 1.0s end padding; with the shipped `config.json` they now get the configured **0.5s**. `run_process_and_stitch.py` already passed config values, so it is unchanged.

## Cross-repo note

`sports-data-collector` `src/parsers/cvat/rally_cvat.py::detect_issues` docstring justified not flagging vendor boundary slack by citing the compiler''s hardcoded 0.5/1.0 padding — companion PR updates that rationale to the config-driven values.

## Verification

- `python run_all_tests.py`: **401 passed** (includes the new regression test)
- ruff + mypy clean on touched files

### 91. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.385Z)

Done. The fix is shipped as two PRs, both CI-green and awaiting your review — I did not merge anything.

**[badminton-highlight-indexer #102](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/102)** — the actual fix:
- Both `VideoCompiler` construction sites in [backend/main.py](backend/main.py) (`/api/compile` around line 508 and the CLI trim path around line 633) now pass `begin_padding`/`end_padding` from the typed `StitchingConfig`, alongside the watermark they already passed. The API site keeps the existing defensive idiom by falling back to `StitchingConfig()` when `global_config` is unset; `StitchingConfig` is now re-exported from `backend.config`.
- New regression test in [tests/test_api_endpoints.py](tests/test_api_endpoints.py) drives the **real** compiler through `/api/compile` with mocked ffmpeg/ffprobe and asserts that a config override of 2.0/3.0 actually moves the trim boundaries to `-ss 8.0 / -to 17.0` for a 10–14s segment — the previously unwired defaults would have produced 9.5/15.0, so this test fails on the old code.
- [docs/CODE_MAP.md](docs/CODE_MAP.md) truth-synced: the stitcher entry now distinguishes the constructor defaults (0.5/1.0) from the config-driven live call sites (0.5/0.5), and the padding recipe row points at the `main.py` wiring.
- Verified: `python run_all_tests.py` → **401 passed**; ruff and mypy clean on touched files; all 4 CI lanes green.

One behavior change worth knowing before merge: live API reels currently get 1.0s of end padding; with the shipped `config.json` they'll get the configured **0.5s** after this lands.

**[sports-data-collector #20](https://github.com/avidullu/sports-data-collector/pull/20)** — the cross-repo coupling you flagged: the `detect_issues` docstring in `rally_cvat.py` now cites the config-driven 0.5/0.5 paddings instead of the hardcoded 0.5/1.0 (and the corrected module path). Docstring-only; collector suite 137 passed, CI green. Your uncommitted `.gitignore` edit there was left untouched.

Both checkouts are back on `master`, and I recorded a checkpoint in the memory current-state file (noting the padding-rationale interaction with the in-flight Roora boundary-slack thread, since that rationale now rests on 0.5s rather than 1.0s of end padding). One observation: an untracked `arial.ttf` appeared at the indexer repo root — not from this work; I left it alone, but it may be a stray from a watermark-font experiment.

### 101. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.385Z)

Done. The rule now lives at the top of your global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, so it loads in every session across all projects:

- **Always `git pull` before starting to read a repo** — at session start for the working directory, and again for any sibling repo (like `sports-data-collector`) the moment work touches it.
- **Safe form only:** `git pull --ff-only` — never auto-merge, rebase, or discard local state to make a pull succeed.
- **If it can't fast-forward** (diverged branch, dirty tree): report the state and proceed read-only on local, flagged as possibly stale — uncommitted changes are treated as a sibling session's or your WIP and never clobbered.

I also captured your *why* in the rule itself — parallel "clean slates" mean master moves between sessions — so future sessions understand the intent, not just the mechanic.

And it paid off immediately: running the pull on both repos just now, both masters were already current, but the fetch surfaced a new remote branch `claude/watermark-bundled-font` from another session — which neatly explains the stray `arial.ttf` I'd flagged at the indexer root earlier (it's that session's watermark-font experiment, left alone).

One option if you ever want this enforced mechanically rather than by instruction: a `SessionStart` hook in `settings.json` could run the `--ff-only` pull automatically before I even wake up. I'd lean against it — a hook pulls blindly without first checking for a dirty tree or a sibling session mid-work on a feature branch, whereas the instruction lets me look before pulling — but say the word and I'll set it up.

1	"""WASB-based hybrid play segmenter.
2	
3	Phase 2 uses **WASB** (nttcom/WASB-SBDT, MIT) shuttle-trajectory detection via
4	WSL2 (backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py). WASB ships pretrained
5	badminton weights and runs today, so this is the live shuttle-motion substrate.
6	
7	For the per-video *calibration* goal we mainly use Phase 2 (trajectory ->
8	candidate windows); the windowing thresholds (velocity_thresh / merge_gap /
9	min_window_duration) are what get tuned against golden rally labels.
10	
11	The pipeline engine (healthcheck -> trajectory -> windows -> AI handoff -> DB)
12	lives in trajectory_hybrid.TrajectoryHybridSegmenter; this class only wires the
13	WASB runner and identity.
14	"""
15	from typing import Any, Dict, Tuple
16	
17	from backend.pipeline.segmenters.base import segmenter_registry
18	from backend.pipeline.segmenters.trajectory_hybrid import TrajectoryHybridSegmenter
19	from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import DetectorRunner

21	
22	
23	class WasbHybridSegmenter(TrajectoryHybridSegmenter):
24	    family = "wasb"
25	    display_label = "WASB"
26	
27	    @property

29	        return "wasb_hybrid"

33	        return ("Two-stage pipeline: WASB shuttle-trajectory detection (WSL2, pretrained badminton "
34	                "weights) for precise candidate rallies + AI provider for semantic boundaries.")
35	
36	    def _build_runner(self, idx_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> Tuple[DetectorRunner, Dict[str, Any]]:
37	        cfg = WasbConfig.from_indexing_cfg(idx_cfg)
38	        return WasbRunner(cfg), {"weights_path": cfg.weights_path}

### 154. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.389Z)

The end-to-end run is **launched and being monitored**. Here's the setup and what to expect:

**What's running** (background job `bwqevfnlg`, log at [output/gx010125_run.log](output/gx010125_run.log)):
1. **MD5 hash** of the 11.5 GB source (this is the `video_id`) — a few minutes.
2. **WASB shuttle-trajectory detection on the GPU** (RTX 2070 Super via WSL2 Ubuntu, pretrained badminton weights — verified present) over the full 12.8-minute 4K60 video → candidate rally windows.
3. **Gemini confirmation** of each candidate window (serial uploads with backoff, re-encoded 1280p chunks) → confirmed rallies **with per-rally `shot_count`** (Gemini's shuttle-exchange count; duration-based estimate only if the model omits it).
4. **Filter + stitch**: only rallies with `shot_count >= 8` (your ">7 shots") survive, then the compiler cuts and concatenates them with the configured paddings and watermark into `output/GX010125_highlights.mp4`.

**Your config question — yes, it's possible, with one nuance.** `stitching.min_shot_count` is honored by the offline runner flow (`run_process_and_stitch.py` lineage), which is what I'm using — I set it to **8** in [config.json](config.json) and the runner reads it from config with no override, logging each segment's shot count and KEEP/drop decision so you can audit the filter. The nuance: the **API path** (`/api/compile`) does *not* apply `min_shot_count` — it stitches exactly the `segment_ids` the caller picks (the UI is expected to filter). If you want the config filter enforced on the API path too, that's a small PR — say the word.

**Config changes made** (local, uncommitted): `indexing.default_segmenter: gemini → wasb_hybrid` (the GPU path you asked for) and `stitching.min_shot_count: 1 → 8`. Also synced to head first — your merge of the padding fix (#102) means this run uses the now config-driven 0.5s paddings.

Expect roughly 30–60+ minutes total (GPU inference on ~46k frames is the long pole). The monitor will report each phase as it lands; I'll summarize the kept rallies and the reel path when it finishes.

### 224. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.395Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Commit, push, and open PR for API min_shot_count enforcement
command: cd C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer && git commit -m @'
feat(api): enforce stitching.min_shot_count on /api/compile

The config shot-count floor was honored only by the offline runner
(run_process_and_stitch.py); /api/compile stitched whatever segment_ids the
caller picked, so config.json stitching.min_shot_count was silently ignored
on the live API path (owner-requested parity, 2026-06-12).

Semantics: segments whose metadata carries a shot_count below the floor are
dropped (logged); segments WITHOUT shot_count metadata are exempt so
explicitly requested manual/legacy segments cannot silently vanish under the
default floor of 1 (every live segmenter always writes shot_count). All
matching segments below the floor -> 400 with the floor in the detail.

Tests: floor drops a below-floor segment while keeping above-floor and
no-metadata segments; all-below-floor returns 400.

Suite: 407 passed; ruff clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin feat/api-min-shot-count && gh pr create --title "feat(api): enforce stitching.min_shot_count on /api/compile" --body @'
## What

`stitching.min_shot_count` was honored only by the offline runner (`run_process_and_stitch.py`); `/api/compile` stitched exactly the `segment_ids` given, silently ignoring the configured shot-count floor. Owner requested parity (2026-06-12): the API path now applies the floor.

## Semantics

- Segments whose metadata has `shot_count < min_shot_count` are **dropped** (count logged).
- Segments **without** `shot_count` metadata are **exempt** — explicitly requested manual/legacy segments must not silently vanish under the default floor of 1. Every live segmenter (gemini, wasb/tracknet/yolo hybrids, motion) always writes `shot_count`, so the floor bites exactly where intended.
- If **all** matching segments fall below the floor → `400` naming the floor value.

## Verification

- New tests: mixed below/above/no-metadata segments → only the below-floor one is dropped; all-below-floor → 400.
- `python run_all_tests.py`: **407 passed**; ruff clean.

Per owner authorization (2026-06-12), this will be merged on green CI as a good-engineering-fix PR.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
'@
[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""WASB shuttle-trajectory inference on an arbitrary frame sequence (runs in WSL).
2	
3	WASB-SBDT only ships *dataset* evaluation; this is a thin wrapper that reuses its
4	own building blocks (ImageDataset transform + detector + online tracker) to run on
5	any folder of extracted frames and emit a trajectory CSV in the exact shape the
6	indexer's `tracknet_runner.parse_trajectory_csv` already consumes:
7	
8	    Frame,Visibility,X,Y
9	
10	It must run INSIDE the WSL `wasb` conda env, from the WASB-SBDT `src/` dir (it
11	imports WASB's `detectors`/`trackers`/`dataloaders`). The Windows-side adapter
12	(`wasb_runner.py`) stages this file + the frames into WSL and invokes it.
13	
14	Resumable across reboots
15	------------------------
16	The GPU detector pass (one forward per sliding window — ~21k for a 6-min clip) is
17	the slow, expensive part; the CPU tracker pass that turns detections into a
18	trajectory is cheap and *stateful* (it must see every frame in order, so it can't
19	resume mid-stream). We exploit that split:
20	
21	  * The detector pass writes its per-window raw outputs incrementally to
22	    ``<cache>/det_raw.jsonl`` (flushed+fsync'd every ``--flush-every`` windows) and
23	    records progress in ``<cache>/manifest.json`` (atomic write). A reboot loses at
24	    most ``--flush-every`` windows of GPU work instead of the whole pass.
25	  * On restart we replay the cached windows, skip the DataLoader ahead to the first
26	    un-cached window, and continue. Once every window is cached, the detector pass
27	    is skipped entirely and we just re-run the (cheap, deterministic) tracker over
28	    the full ordered cache -> trajectory CSV. So a lost trajectory CSV costs seconds,
29	    not the GPU hour.
30	
31	The cache is keyed by the output path (``<out>_wasbcache/`` by default), lives next
32	to the output (NOT in %TEMP%), and is invalidated automatically if the frames dir,
33	weights, sport, window size, or frame count change.
34	
35	Usage (in WSL, from ~/models/WASB-SBDT/src):
36	    python wasb_infer.py --frames_dir /path/frames --weights ../pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar \
37	        --out /path/traj.csv [--sport badminton] [--limit N] [--cache-dir DIR] [--flush-every 1000] [--fresh]
38	"""

40	import os.path as osp
41	import csv
42	import glob
43	import json
44	import argparse
45	import logging
46	from collections import defaultdict
47	
48	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
49	
50	CACHE_VERSION = 1
51	_DET_FILE = "det_raw.jsonl"
52	_MANIFEST_FILE = "manifest.json"
53	
54	
55	def _build_cfg(weights: str, sport: str):
56	    """Compose the WASB eval config via Hydra, overriding model/weights/device."""
57	    from hydra import compose, initialize
58	    with initialize(config_path="configs", version_base=None):
59	        cfg = compose(config_name="eval", overrides=[
60	            f"dataset={sport}",
61	            "model=wasb",
62	            f"detector.model_path={weights}",
63	            "runner.device=cuda",
64	            "runner.gpus=[0]",      # this box has a single GPU; default config assumes more
65	        ])
66	    return cfg
67	
68	
69	def _frame_id(path: str) -> int:
70	    """Best-effort integer frame id from a frame filename (e.g. frame_000180.png -> 180)."""
71	    stem = osp.splitext(osp.basename(path))[0]
72	    digits = "".join(ch for ch in stem if ch.isdigit())
73	    return int(digits) if digits else -1
74	
75	
76	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
77	# Resumable detector cache (pure-Python, no torch/numpy — unit-testable)
78	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
79	def _cache_paths(cache_dir: str):
80	    return osp.join(cache_dir, _DET_FILE), osp.join(cache_dir, _MANIFEST_FILE)
81	
82	
83	def default_cache_dir(out_csv: str) -> str:
84	    """Stable cache dir next to the trajectory output (survives reboots; not %TEMP%)."""
85	    d = osp.dirname(osp.abspath(out_csv))

### 285. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.399Z)

Now I have the real root cause. Let me lay out what the evidence shows:

- **No reboot** (last boot June 11) and **no sleep (S3) events** — but a chain of `Kernel-Power` **session transitions** ("transitioned from 51 to 52", reasons `InputHid`/`SessionUn`) around 22:53 last night. That's **Modern Standby (connected standby)**: when the machine sits idle with the screen off, Windows enters S0 low-power and the **Desktop Activity Moderator suspends Win32 processes and pauses the WSL2 VM**. That froze the Python orchestrator and the WSL inference mid-run — no clean exit, no timeout logged, frame cache frozen at exactly 30,087. `standby-timeout=0` doesn't prevent this; Modern Standby is screen-off-triggered.

The crucial good news from reading `wasb_infer.py`: the **GPU detector pass is checkpointed and resumable** (`det_raw.jsonl` + manifest, fsync'd every 1000 windows), and frame extraction is idempotent once complete. The killer was purely the machine idling — not a code bug.

Let me gather the exact numbers I need to choose the fastest extraction path, and install a keep-awake so this can't happen again.

### 286. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.399Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Install keep-awake wake lock to block Modern Standby, persistent for session
command: $sig = @'
using System;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
public class Caffeine {
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true)]
  public static extern uint SetThreadExecutionState(uint esFlags);
}
'@
Add-Type -TypeDefinition $sig
# ES_CONTINUOUS(0x80000000) | ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED(0x1) | ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED(0x2)
$r = [Caffeine]::SetThreadExecutionState([uint32]"0x80000003")
Write-Output "SetThreadExecutionState returned: $r (nonzero = success)"
Write-Output "--- powercfg requests (who is holding wake locks) ---"
powercfg /requests
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 318. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.401Z)

Good — ffmpeg is extracting cleanly at ~52 fps, and I've confirmed the stitch line cuts from the **4K original** (`SOURCE_PATH`), not the proxy. Here's the full picture:

## What happened overnight

It wasn't a code bug — it was the **machine, twice**. The Kernel-Power event log shows **Modern Standby (connected standby)** transitions around 22:53 last night. When the machine sits idle with the screen off, Windows enters S0 low-power and **suspends Win32 processes and pauses the WSL2 VM**. That froze the Python orchestrator + WSL inference mid-run, with no clean exit (which is exactly why you saw no error — the log just stopped at "Running WASB inference" and the frame cache froze at 30,087). Note `standby-timeout=0` does *not* prevent this; Modern Standby is screen-off-triggered.

I also discovered *why it kept dying at the same place*: the run **never even reached the GPU** — it was still in **cv2 frame extraction** (the slow part — `wasb_infer.py`'s own docstring warns "cv2 PNG extraction is slow; for big clips prefer ffmpeg"). 46,080 frames at ~20 fps via cv2 = ~38 min of fragile single-threaded extraction, and standby kept killing it before inference began.

## Fixes applied

1. **Root cause — keep-awake.** A persistent wake-lock process (`SetThreadExecutionState(ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED|ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED)`, re-asserted every 60s) is now holding the machine in S0 working state so standby can't suspend the run.
2. **The bottleneck — ffmpeg pre-extraction.** Extracting all 46,080 PNGs with ffmpeg natively inside WSL right now (~15 min, running). The frame count is provably exact (768.768s × 59.94fps = 46,080, CFR — matches cv2's count precisely), so the pipeline's slow cv2 step is **skipped entirely** and it goes straight to GPU inference (which is itself checkpointed/resumable).
3. **Stitch-from-original — confirmed.** The runner indexes the 1080p proxy but cuts the final reel from `F:\…\GX010125.MP4` (the 4K original) — same timeline, so timestamps transfer 1:1. I'll `ffprobe` the output to confirm it's 3840×2160 before calling it done.

Also: the **API `min_shot_count` fix you authorized is merged** ([#107](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/107), squashed to master on green CI).

## What's next (automatic)

When extraction finishes (~15 min), I'll launch the pipeline — it'll skip extraction → **GPU WASB inference** → candidate windows → **Gemini rally confirmation** (serial, your API key's in `.env`) → **filter to shot_count > 7** → **stitch from 4K**. ETA after extraction: ~30–50 min. The live log stays at:

```powershell
Get-Content -Wait -Tail 40 C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer\output\gx010125_run.log
```

I'll report at each milestone and won't go idle on you.

196	        handoff_dir = os.path.join("output", f"chunks_{provider_name}_handoff")
197	        os.makedirs(handoff_dir, exist_ok=True)
198	        logger.info("Phase 3: %s validation on %d candidate windows...",
199	                    provider_name, len(all_candidate_windows))
200	
201	        def process_candidate_window(wi: int, window: Dict[str, Any]) -> List[dict]:
202	            w_start = max(0, window["start"] - handoff_padding)
203	            w_end = min(video_duration, window["end"] + handoff_padding)
204	            w_dur = w_end - w_start
205	            w_path = os.path.join(handoff_dir, f"handoff_{video_id}_{wi}.mp4")
206	            if not extract_video_chunk(video_path, w_start, w_dur, w_path, reencode=True):
207	                return []
208	            prompt = sport_profile.get_prompt(chunk_duration=w_dur)
209	            segments, _ = provider.analyze_video(w_path, prompt, chunk_duration=w_dur,
210	                                                 label=f"Handoff {wi+1}")
211	            valid = []
212	            for seg in segments:
213	                try:
214	                    seg["start_time"] = float(seg["start_time"]) + w_start
215	                    seg["end_time"] = float(seg["end_time"]) + w_start
216	                    seg["_candidate_id"] = candidate_ids[wi]
217	                    valid.append(seg)
218	                except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError) as e:
219	                    logger.warning("Dropping segment with unparseable timestamps: %s — %s", seg, e)
220	            try:
221	                os.remove(w_path)
222	            except OSError:
223	                pass
224	            return valid
225	
226	        ai_segments: List[dict] = []
227	        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=ai_max_workers) as executor:
228	            futures = [executor.submit(process_candidate_window, wi, cw)
229	                       for wi, cw in enumerate(all_candidate_windows)]
230	            for future in futures:
231	                ai_segments.extend(future.result())
232	        try:
233	            os.rmdir(handoff_dir)
234	        except OSError:
235	            pass
236	        logger.info("Phase 3 completed. AI confirmed %d semantic play segments.", len(ai_segments))
237	
238	        # --- Phase 4: DB population + candidate linking ---
239	        segments_data = []
240	        for segment in ai_segments:
241	            try:
242	                start = float(segment["start_time"])
243	                end = float(segment["end_time"])
244	                dur = end - start
245	                metadata = {
246	                    "shot_count": self._shot_count_from(segment, dur),
247	                    "shot_count_confidence": segment.get("shot_count_confidence", "Unknown"),
248	                    "detector": f"{self.family}-hybrid-{model_name}",
249	                }
250	                segment_id = self.db.add_play_segment(video_id, start, end, None, None, metadata)
251	                if segment_id:
252	                    candidate_id = segment.get("_candidate_id")
253	                    if candidate_id is not None:
254	                        self.db.link_candidate_to_segment(candidate_id, segment_id)
255	                    segments_data.append({
256	                        "id": segment_id, "start_time": start, "end_time": end,
257	                        "duration": dur, "metadata": metadata,
258	                    })
259	            except Exception as e:
260	                logger.warning("Skipping invalid segment struct: %s", e)
261	
262	        return segments_data

39	        """Make a filesystem path safe to embed inside single quotes in an ffmpeg
40	        filtergraph: forward slashes (Windows-friendly) + escaped single quotes."""
41	        return path.replace("\\", "/").replace("'", r"\'")

description: Commit, push, open PR for watermark colon fix
command: cd C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer && git commit -m @'
fix(stitcher): escape drive-letter colon in ffmpeg filtergraph paths

The on-by-default bundled-font watermark (PR #103) was silently disabled on
Windows: _BUNDLED_FONT is an absolute path (C:\...\RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf), and
_ff_quote only converted backslashes + escaped single quotes. ffmpeg's
filtergraph option parser treats ':' as the option separator EVEN inside single
quotes, so `fontfile='C:/...'` truncates the operand at 'C' and the per-clip
encode fails with "Invalid argument". The compiler's fallback then re-encoded
every clip WITHOUT the watermark — branding off, no error surfaced to the user.

Reproduced + fix verified against real ffmpeg (drawtext fontfile AND the logo
movie= source both fail on a raw colon, both succeed with it escaped). Fix:
_ff_quote also escapes ':' -> '\:'. This repairs all three operands that flow
through it (drawtext fontfile, textfile, movie= logo). POSIX paths (incl. the
Linux bundled-font path) have no colon, so the change is a no-op there.

Surfaced live during a real GPU highlight run on a 4K GoPro match (12 of 12
clips logged "watermark encode failed; retrying without it").

Tests: _ff_quote escapes the drive-letter colon (and handles colon+quote+
backslash together); _watermark_filter emits fontfile='C\:/...' not 'C:/...'.
Suite 409 green; ruff + mypy clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ && git push -u origin fix/watermark-windows-colon && gh pr create --title "fix(stitcher): escape drive-letter colon in ffmpeg filtergraph paths" --body @'
## What

The on-by-default bundled-font watermark (added in [#103](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/103)) was **silently disabled on Windows**. `_BUNDLED_FONT` is an absolute path (`C:\…\RobotoSlab-Regular.ttf`), and `_ff_quote` only converted `\`→`/` and escaped `'`. But ffmpeg''s filtergraph parser treats `:` as the option separator **even inside single quotes**, so `fontfile='C:/…'` truncates the operand at `C` and the per-clip encode dies with `Invalid argument`. The compiler''s graceful fallback then re-encoded every clip **without** the watermark — so branding was off and no error reached the user.

## Root cause + fix, verified against real ffmpeg

Reproduced with a minimal `drawtext` encode: raw `fontfile='C:/…'` → `Invalid argument`; escaped `fontfile='C\:/…'` → exit 0. Same for the logo `movie='C:/…'` source (fails on `'C'`, succeeds escaped). So absolute logo paths were broken on Windows too.

Fix: `_ff_quote` now also escapes `:` → `\:`. One change repairs all three operands that flow through it (`drawtext fontfile`, `textfile`, `movie=`). POSIX paths (including the Linux bundled-font path) contain no colon, so it''s a no-op there — and `textfile=` only worked before because its temp path was relative (no drive letter).

## How it surfaced

Live, during a real GPU highlight run on a 4K GoPro match — all 12 clips logged `watermark encode failed; retrying without it`.

## Tests

- `_ff_quote` escapes the drive-letter colon, leaves POSIX paths unchanged, and handles colon+quote+backslash together.
- `_watermark_filter` emits `fontfile='C\:/…'`, never the raw `C:/…`.
- `python run_all_tests.py`: **409 passed**; ruff + mypy clean.

Per standing authorization (2026-06-12), merging good-engineering-fix PRs on green CI.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

Everything's complete and recorded. Here's the wrap-up.

## ✅ Your highlights: `output\GX010125_highlights.mp4`

**11 rally highlights, every one with more than 7 shots** (8, 8, 9, 9, 10, 10, 11, 11, 12, 14, 16 shots), selected from 52 rallies detected across your 12.8-minute match.

- **True 4K, from the original**: 3840×2160 h264 59.94fps, 2:23, 1.15 GB — cut from `F:\…\GX010125.MP4`, not the proxy (the 4K resolution is the proof). Config paddings applied (0.5s/0.5s).
- **GPU end-to-end**: WASB shuttle-tracking on your RTX 2070 Super via WSL (64.8% visibility) → 18 candidate windows → Gemini confirmed 52 rallies with shot counts → config `min_shot_count=8` filter. A 7-shot rally was correctly dropped, confirming ">7" semantics.

## The config is picked up — and now enforced everywhere

`stitching.min_shot_count` drove the filter (you can see each rally's KEEP/drop in `output\gx010125_run.log`). I also **merged [#107](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/107)** so the **API path** (`/api/compile`) enforces the same floor — previously it ignored the config and stitched whatever segment IDs it was given.

## Two things fixed along the way (both merged on green CI, per your standing authorization)

1. **[#107](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/107)** — API `min_shot_count` parity (above).
2. **[#108](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/108)** — a real bug the run surfaced: the on-by-default watermark (bundled font from #103) was **silently failing on Windows** — the absolute font path's drive-letter colon (`C:/…`) broke ffmpeg's drawtext parser, so every clip fell back to no-watermark. Fixed by escaping the colon in `_ff_quote` (also repaired absolute logo paths). Verified against real ffmpeg, with regression tests.

## Why the earlier attempts died (not a code bug)

**Modern Standby** suspended the machine overnight twice, freezing the run mid-inference. Fixed with a keep-awake lock. I also found the actual bottleneck was **cv2 frame extraction** (~38 min, where it kept dying — *before* the GPU even started), so I pre-extract with ffmpeg now (count-exact for CFR) and the full run completed in one ~47-min pass. The whole recipe is saved to memory for next time.

### 554. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.420Z)

Both your questions answered, the flowchart is above, and the ≥5 reel is now building (reusing cache — no WASB/Gemini re-run).

## 1. Resolution: Gemini saw **1080p**, not 4K

The pipeline deliberately splits resolution by stage:
- **WASB + Gemini operated on the 1080p proxy.** The candidate clips uploaded to Gemini were `extract_video_chunk(<1080p proxy>, …, reencode=True)` with no further downscaling — so 1080p reached Gemini. This is the right trade: shot-counting and boundary detection don't need 4K, and it keeps WASB decode and Gemini upload fast/cheap.
- **The 4K original was used only for the final stitch** — that's why your reel came out true 3840×2160 while the analysis ran on 1080p.

## 2. Flowchart — where WASB sits, how "rally end" is decided

The diagram above traces it, but the key idea is **rally boundaries are decided in two passes** (the two purple/pink-flagged nodes):

- **WASB (coarse, motion-based)** — runs on the GPU over the 1080p frames and produces a per-frame shuttle trajectory `(x, y, visible)`. `trajectory_to_action_windows` turns that into candidate rally windows: **shuttle moving = in-rally**; short pauses are fused (`dead_time_merge_gap`), and a long stretch of stillness/invisibility ends the window. That's the *approximate* "where could a rally be" — it found 18 windows.
- **Gemini (fine, semantic)** — each candidate window is cut to a padded 1080p clip and sent to Gemini, which watches the actual play and returns the **exact** rally start/end (start = racket-to-shuttle contact, end = grounding/fault) plus the `shot_count`. One WASB window can contain several real rallies — that's how 18 windows became 52 confirmed rallies.

So: **WASB proposes, Gemini disposes.** The shot count that your filter uses is Gemini's `shuttle_exchange_count`.

## 3. The ≥5 reel is building now

`output\GX010125_min5_stitch.log` is the live log. It's reusing the **24 cached rallies with shot_count ≥ 5** (vs 11 at >7) straight from the DB — **no WASB or Gemini re-run** — and cutting from the 4K original. Output will be `output\GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4`. ETA ~8–12 min (24 clips re-encoded at 4K).

One note: this reel **will have the watermark** ("Generated by Khelsutra.guru"), because the bundled-font fix (#108) is now in place and the config default is on — so it also doubles as live confirmation that fix works on real 4K. Say the word if you'd prefer it without and I'll regenerate watermark-free in a couple minutes. I'll confirm the count, duration, and 4K resolution when it lands.

1	"""TrackNet shuttle-trajectory runner (WSL2 subprocess adapter).
2	
3	This bridges the Windows-side indexer to a TensorFlow TrackNetV4 install living
4	inside WSL2 (ADR-001 in docs/DECISIONS.md). The flow is:
5	
6	    1. Translate the Windows video path to its WSL-visible form (and optionally
7	       stage it into the WSL filesystem to avoid slow /mnt/c per-frame reads).
8	    2. Invoke ``src/predict.py`` inside the TrackNetV4 conda env via ``wsl``.
9	    3. Read back the per-frame ``Frame,Visibility,X,Y`` CSV it writes.
10	    4. Convert that trajectory into high-motion "action windows" — the same
11	       candidate-rally shape HybridYoloSegmenter produces — so the rest of the
12	       pipeline (AI handoff, DB population) is unchanged.
13	
14	Nothing here imports TensorFlow; all model code stays in WSL.
15	"""
16	
17	import os
18	import csv
19	import shlex
20	import logging
21	import subprocess
22	from dataclasses import dataclass
23	from typing import List, Dict, Any, Optional, Tuple

25	from backend.pipeline.detectors.base import DetectorRunner, WslCommandMixin, wsl_tilde_quote
26	
27	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
28	
29	
30	@dataclass
31	class TrackNetConfig:
32	    """Resolved settings for a TrackNet WSL run (built from config.json -> indexing.tracknet)."""
33	    distro: str = "Ubuntu"
34	    repo_dir: str = "~/models/TrackNetV4"          # WSL path to the cloned repo
35	    conda_sh: str = "~/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh"
36	    conda_env: str = "TrackNetV4"
37	    weights_path: str = ""                          # WSL path to the .h5/.keras weights (REQUIRED)
38	    queue_length: int = 5
39	    stage_in_wsl: bool = True                       # copy video into WSL fs first (perf)
40	    wsl_stage_dir: str = "~/clips"                  # where staged videos/outputs live in WSL
41	    timeout_sec: int = 1800                         # 30 min; kills a hung call (0 = no timeout)
42	
43	    @classmethod
44	    def from_indexing_cfg(cls, idx_cfg: Dict[str, Any]) -> "TrackNetConfig":
45	        tn = (idx_cfg or {}).get("tracknet", {}) or {}
46	        return cls(

59	def to_wsl_mnt_path(win_path: str) -> str:
60	    """Translate a Windows path (C:\\Users\\x) to its WSL /mnt form (/mnt/c/Users/x).
61	
62	    Already-POSIX paths (starting with / or ~) are returned unchanged so the
63	    same helper is safe to call on either kind of path.
64	    """
65	    p = str(win_path)
66	    if p.startswith("/") or p.startswith("~"):
67	        return p
68	    p = p.replace("\\", "/")
69	    if len(p) >= 2 and p[1] == ":":
70	        drive = p[0].lower()
71	        return f"/mnt/{drive}{p[2:]}"
72	    return p

### 563. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.421Z)

Now I can give you the full picture with exact pointers. Pinning links to the current commit (`6de70aa`) so the line numbers stay stable.

## Where `trajectory_to_action_windows` comes from

It's **the indexer's own code** — not from WASB or TrackNet upstream. It was written as part of the **TrackNet WSL adapter** (ADR-001), and is deliberately **model-agnostic**: it lives as a `@staticmethod` on `TrackNetRunner`, and `WasbRunner` reuses it verbatim because both detectors emit the **same `Frame,Visibility,X,Y` trajectory CSV**:

- [`tracknet_runner.py::trajectory_to_action_windows`](backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py:233) — the windowing logic → [GitHub L233–L301](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py#L233-L301)
- [`tracknet_runner.py::parse_trajectory_csv`](backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py:214) — CSV → points → [GitHub L214–L231](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/tracknet_runner.py#L214-L231)
- [`wasb_runner.py`](backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py:154) — WASB inheriting both, the "model-agnostic" seam → [GitHub L154–L156](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_runner.py#L154-L156)

## How the *trajectory* itself was detected (the `(x, y, visible)` per frame)

That's WASB, wrapped in [`wasb_infer.py::run`](backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py:207) ([GitHub](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L207-L345)). Two upstream building blocks from **[nttcom/WASB-SBDT](https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT)** (MIT; "Widely Applicable Strong Baseline for Sports Ball Detection & Tracking", Tarashima et al., BMVC 2023):

1. **Detector** (`build_detector` → `detector.run_tensor`, [L297](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L297)): a heatmap CNN (HRNet-style backbone). It takes a **short stack of consecutive frames** (`frames_in`, here 3 — that's why 46,080 frames → 46,078 windows) so it can use motion, and predicts a Gaussian **heatmap** per frame. The peak of the heatmap = the shuttle's pixel location; peak confidence below threshold ⇒ "not visible" that frame.
2. **Online tracker** (`build_tracker` → `tracker.update`, [L338](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/blob/6de70aa5c840beb91aa50500e6e04b4d812f7429/backend/pipeline/detectors/wasb_infer.py#L338)): links the per-frame peaks into one coherent track (position/motion association), rejecting spurious blobs and filling `Visibility=0, X=Y=-1` where the shuttle is genuinely lost.

The result is one row per frame — `Frame, Visibility(0/1), X, Y` — which is exactly what `parse_trajectory_csv` reads. (Your run: 46,080 rows, 29,879 visible = 64.8%.)

## How `trajectory_to_action_windows` turns that into candidate rallies

The core is a **velocity gate + temporal grouping**:

```python
# per consecutive VISIBLE pair: speed as fraction-of-frame-width per second
dist     = ((p.x - prev.x)**2 + (p.y - prev.y)**2) ** 0.5
velocity = (dist / frame_width) * (fps / dframes)
if velocity > velocity_thresh:        # shuttle is "in flight"
    active.append((p.frame, velocity))
# an INVISIBLE frame resets prev → absent shuttle = not in flight (a natural break)
```
```python
# group active frames; a gap longer than merge_gap closes the window
max_gap_frames = int(merge_gap * fps)
if af.frame - group[-1].frame <= max_gap_frames:
    group.append(af)        # same rally — short pause tolerated
else:
    windows.append(_finalize(group)); group = [af]   # new rally
...
return [w for w in windows if (w.end - w.start) >= min_window_duration]
```

Step by step:
1. **Velocity** is normalized by frame width (so it's resolution-independent — works identically on 1080p) and converted to per-second. Above `velocity_thresh` ⇒ the frame is "active" (shuttle flying).
2. **An invisible shuttle breaks the trajectory** (`prev = None`) — disappearance is itself a boundary signal.
3. **Grouping** merges active frames within `merge_gap` seconds into one window — so the brief pauses *between shots within a rally* don't split it, but a long stillness (shuttle down, players resetting) **ends** the window. ← this is the "coarse rally boundary" from the flowchart.
4. Windows shorter than `min_window_duration` are dropped (kills jitter/false blips). Each window carries `start/end`, `peak_velocity`, `mean_velocity`, `active_frame_count`.

These three knobs come from `config.json → indexing.wasb`:
- `velocity_threshold` (0.02) → `velocity_thresh`
- `dead_time_merge_gap` (2.0s) → `merge_gap`
- `min_action_window_duration` (2.0s) → `min_window_duration`

So WASB gives you *motion-derived* candidate windows (18 of them on your video); Gemini then refines each into precise rallies with shot counts. The windowing is intentionally the same shape `HybridYoloSegmenter` produces, which is why the AI-handoff + DB stages are detector-agnostic.

---

### 587. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.424Z)

## Your GPU right now: idle of pipeline work

`32% util, 2.2 GB, 21 W` — that's just **desktop apps** holding the GPU for compositing/decode (Edge WebView, explorer, WhatsApp, the Claude app, VS Code, and **VLC** — possibly you previewing the reel). **No compute job is running** — no `python` or `ffmpeg` process, and no ML/encode load (the 2070 Super pulls ~80 W under real load; 21 W is idle). The ≥5 stitch already finished.

## Does stitching use the GPU? **No — it's CPU.**

The compiler encodes with the **software x264 encoder**, not NVENC:
- Per-clip re-encode: [`-c:v libx264 -preset superfast`](backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py:248) (CPU)
- Final join: [`-c copy`](backend/pipeline/stitcher/compiler.py:332) (stream-copy, no encoding at all)

So the ~11-minute ≥5 stitch was **CPU-bound** (24 clips of 4K x264 + a watermark drawtext filter, which is also a CPU filter). That's why GPU stayed low while it ran.

**For contrast, what *did* run on the GPU earlier:**
- The **4K→1080p proxy transcode** — `h264_nvenc` (GPU encoder).
- **WASB shuttle inference** — the detector CNN on CUDA.

So in this whole job, the GPU did the *analysis* (WASB) and the *proxy*, while the *final stitch* is CPU.

## ≥5 reel is done and verified: `output\GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4`

- **24 rallies** (shot_count ≥ 5), **true 4K** 3840×2160, 59.94 fps, **4:08**, 1987 MB, cut from the original.
- **0 watermark failures** this time (vs 12/12 on the >7 reel) — so the watermark is now correctly burned in. That's a clean end-to-end confirmation that the #108 fix works on real 4K footage.

You now have both: `GX010125_highlights.mp4` (11 rallies, >7 shots, no watermark) and `GX010125_highlights_min5.mp4` (24 rallies, ≥5 shots, watermarked).

---

If the 11-minute CPU stitch felt slow: I can make the stitcher **GPU-encode with NVENC** (decode `-hwaccel cuda` + `-c:v h264_nvenc`), which would cut 4K stitching from ~minutes to ~tens of seconds. It's a legit improvement, but it needs a **software fallback** (NVENC isn't present on the Linux CI or non-NVIDIA machines) and a CRF→CQ quality mapping, so I'd do it as a proper PR with the fallback rather than hard-switching. Want me to take that on?

### 602. user (2026-07-02T06:11:33.425Z)

can you make a plan on how to ensure we keep doing cleanups in a healthy way?

### 603. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.425Z)

Here's a plan for keeping cache hygiene healthy without it becoming a chore — built around what we actually saw accumulate.

## The core problem

The bloat is **regenerable intermediates**, dominated by one thing: PNG frame extraction materializes every frame to disk — **93 GB for a single 12.8-min 4K video**. Across many videos that's unbounded. Staged video copies (2 GB+ each) pile on. Meanwhile the *valuable* outputs are tiny or already archived. So the principle is simple:

> **Separate regenerable intermediates (purge aggressively, automatically) from durable outputs (keep/archive). Make purging default-safe — dry-run first, the "show me before you delete" pattern.**

## Retention policy — what lives where, and its lifespan

| Artifact | Location | Regenerable? | Policy |
|---|---|---|---|
| `*_frames/` (PNG decode) | WSL `~/clips` | ✅ from video | **Delete on success** (only needed during the detector pass) |
| Staged `*.mp4` copies | WSL `~/clips` | ✅ from original | **Delete on success** |
| `*_wasbcache/` (resume cache) | WSL + `output/wasb` | ✅ | Keep only while a run is in-flight/iterating; drop once CSV exists |
| `chunks_*_handoff/`, `tmp*/` | Windows `output/` | ✅ | Auto-clean per run; sweep stragglers on crash |
| 1080p proxy `*_1080p.mp4` | Windows `output/` | ✅ from 4K | Keep while iterating on that video; purge when done |
| **Trajectory CSVs** | `~/clips`, `output/wasb` | ⚠️ GPU-only | **Keep**; archive to `Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories` |
| **SQLite DB (rallies)** | `output/` | ⚠️ Gemini cost | **Keep** — this is what makes re-stitch free |
| **Final reels** | `output/` | ⚠️ expensive | Keep until copied out, then purge |

## Tier 1 — Make the pipeline self-clean (the real fix)

The highest-leverage change: **delete the `*_frames/` dir and staged video automatically once the trajectory CSV is written successfully.** Frames are purely intermediate — after the CSV exists they're dead weight. Done right this caps per-video frame bloat to "during the run only," so it never accumulates again.

- Add `indexing.keep_frames: false` (default) to config; gate deletion on it so debugging can opt to retain.
- **Only delete on success** (CSV present) — on failure, keep frames + `wasbcache` so the resume-across-reboots design still works.
- Wire it in `wasb_runner.run_predict` / `tracknet_runner` (they already know the frames path). This is a clean, testable PR.

## Tier 2 — A dry-run-first cleanup tool

Replace ad-hoc `rm` with `tools/cleanup_caches.py` (or scratch first):
- Scans **both** WSL `~/clips` and Windows `output/{wasb,chunks_*_handoff,tmp*}`.
- Classifies each entry (regenerable vs durable) and prints a **sized report — dry-run by default** (exactly the review-first flow you just used).
- `--apply` to delete; `--keep-video <id>` to protect current work; `--older-than 7d` / `--min-size` filters.
- One command, repeatable, no per-session re-discovery of paths.

## Tier 3 — Cadence + guardrails (so it actually happens)

- **Disk preflight in the runner**: check `df` before extraction; warn (or refuse) if free space < threshold (e.g. 100 GB), optionally offering to GC the oldest frame caches.
- **Cadence tied to the existing checkpoint convention**: run the cleanup tool's dry-run at session wrap and after each completed video; that's when frames are safe to drop (CSV + DB rallies exist).
- **Optional passive hook**: a `Stop`/`SessionEnd` hook in `settings.json` that runs the dry-run *report only* (never auto-deletes) so you always see accumulation before it's a problem.

## Tier 4 — Guarantee outputs are durable first

Cleanup is only safe because outputs survive: ensure trajectory CSVs land in `Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories` and reels get copied out, *then* intermediates are always disposable. This is already the convention — Tier 1/2 just lean on it.

---

**My recommendation:** Tier 1 is the one that actually ends the problem (the pipeline stops hoarding 93 GB/video); Tier 2 makes the occasional manual sweep safe and fast. Tiers 3–4 are policy/cadence, mostly already in your conventions.

Want me to:

### 732. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.433Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Commit and push cache-hygiene branch
command: cd C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer && git commit -m @'
feat: cache hygiene — self-cleaning pipeline + cleanup tool + disk guard

The GPU pipeline left ~GBs of regenerable intermediates per video in the WSL
stage dir — dominated by PNG frame extraction (~93 GB for one 12.8-min 4K clip).
Across videos this grew unbounded (observed live: ~163 GB in ~/clips). The
valuable outputs (trajectory CSVs, the rally DB, final reels) are tiny or already
archived, so the fix is to treat frames/staged copies as disposable and purge them
automatically + safely.

Tier 1 — self-cleaning runners (the root fix):
- After a SUCCESSFUL run (trajectory CSV written) the WASB runner deletes the
  decoded frame cache + staged video; TrackNet deletes its staged video. Gated by
  indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.keep_frames (default false). On FAILURE nothing is
  deleted, so the frame + detector caches remain for a resumable re-run.

Tier 3a — disk guard:
- indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.min_free_gb (default 0 = off): warn before a run if WSL

### 734. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.433Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: PowerShell]
description: Open the cache-hygiene PR
command: cd C:\Users\avidu\Projects\badminton-highlight-indexer && gh pr create --title "feat: cache hygiene — self-cleaning pipeline + cleanup tool + disk guard" --body @'
## Why

The GPU pipeline left ~GBs of regenerable intermediates per video in the WSL stage dir, dominated by PNG frame extraction (**~93 GB for one 12.8-min 4K clip**). Across videos it grew unbounded — observed live at **~163 GB** in `~/clips`. The valuable outputs (trajectory CSVs, the rally DB, final reels) are tiny or already archived, so the fix is to treat frames/staged copies as disposable and purge them automatically and safely.

## Tier 1 — self-cleaning runners (root fix)

After a **successful** run (trajectory CSV written), the WASB runner deletes the decoded frame cache + staged video; TrackNet deletes its staged video. Gated by `indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.keep_frames` (default **false**). On **failure** nothing is deleted, so the frame cache + detector resume cache remain for a resumable re-run.

## Tier 3a — disk guard

`indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.min_free_gb` (default 0 = off): warn before a run if WSL free space is below the threshold, so a long extraction can't quietly fill the disk. New shared `WslCommandMixin._wsl_free_gb` / `_wsl_rm_rf` helpers (both runners reuse them).

## Tier 2 — `tools/cleanup_caches.py` (dry-run-first)

Scans WSL `~/clips` + Windows `output/`, classifies each entry as **regenerable** (frame caches, staged/proxy videos, handoff clips, temp dirs) vs **durable** (trajectory CSVs, DB, reels), and reports reclaimable space. **Dry-run by default**; `--apply` to delete, `--keep-video <id>`, `--older-than N`, `--include-wasbcache`, `--summary` (one-line nudge for hooks).

Note: the WSL scan uses `du` + `find` arg-globbing — the user's login shell mangles `for` loops (loop var comes back empty), so that pattern is deliberately avoided.

## Tests

`classify` + `plan_deletions` rules (the destructive decision path); runner self-clean on success / retained with `keep_frames`; `_wsl_rm_rf` + `_wsl_free_gb` + config wiring.

- `python run_all_tests.py`: **421 passed** (was 409)
- `ruff check .` + `mypy backend training`: clean

Per standing authorization (2026-06-12), merging good-engineering-fix PRs on green CI.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
'@

### 773. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.435Z)

All four pieces are shipped. Here's what now keeps your caches healthy, automatically.

## What landed — [PR #109](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/109) (merged, CI green, suite 421✓)

**Tier 1 — self-cleaning pipeline (the root fix).** After a *successful* run (trajectory CSV written), the WASB runner now deletes the frame cache + staged video; TrackNet deletes its staged copy. Gated by `indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.keep_frames` (default **false**). On *failure* nothing is deleted, so a re-run still resumes. This is what actually ends the 93 GB/video accumulation — it never builds up again by default.

**Tier 2 — `tools/cleanup_caches.py` (dry-run-first).**
```
python -m tools.cleanup_caches                    # report only (safe)
python -m tools.cleanup_caches --apply            # delete the purge list
python -m tools.cleanup_caches --keep-video GX010125   # protect one video
python -m tools.cleanup_caches --older-than 7     # only >7-day-old cruft
```
Scans WSL `~/clips` + Windows `output/`, classifies regenerable (frames, staged/proxy videos, handoff/temp) vs durable (trajectory CSVs, DB, reels), reports reclaimable space. `--apply`, `--keep-video`, `--older-than`, `--include-wasbcache` all supported.

**Tier 3 — disk guard + hook.**
- `indexing.{wasb,tracknet}.min_free_gb` (default 0=off): warns before a run if WSL space is low.
- A **SessionStart hook** in `.claude/settings.local.json` (local, gitignored) runs `--summary` and surfaces a one-line nudge **only when >5 GB is reclaimable** — silent otherwise, so no nagging. It activates next session start (right now it would report ~96 GB, since GX010125's 93 GB frames are still on disk by your choice).

**Policy saved to memory** → `cache-hygiene-policy.md` (retention table + tooling + the `for e in *` WSL footgun I hit), indexed in MEMORY.md, with a checkpoint in current-state.

## Two honest notes
- I verified the hook config (valid JSON, permissions preserved, command pipe-tested) but **couldn't fire a SessionStart hook mid-turn** — it'll run at your next session start. If it doesn't, opening `/hooks` once reloads the watcher.
- A real footgun surfaced while building the tool: your **login shell mangles `for e in *` loops** (the loop variable comes back empty), which silently broke my first WSL scanner. The shipped tool uses `du`+`find` arg-globbing instead, and I documented it in memory so it doesn't bite a future session.

Net effect: you shouldn't have to think about this again — the pipeline self-cleans, the guard warns you before disk pressure, and the session nudge catches anything that slips through. The one-time `python -m tools.cleanup_caches --apply` whenever you want will sweep the pre-existing backlog (including GX010125's frames once you're done with it).

### 785. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.436Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	# Badminton Highlight & Rally Indexer
2	
3	A local, AI-powered web application and CLI utility designed to process long-form badminton recordings (e.g., from GoPro), run comprehensive pluggable validations (quality, integrity, and relevance checks), index all rallies into a relational SQLite database, and dynamically compile custom highlight reels using high-speed FFmpeg stitching.
4	
5	The pipeline is built around three pluggable registries — **validators**, **segmenters**, and **AI providers** — plus a **sport profile** registry so the same engine can target other racket/court sports in the future. Badminton is the default profile.
6	
7	> **Where this is heading:** the project is evolving from an AI-API-dependent prototype into a self-improving tool that learns rally detection *offline* from a commercially-licensed, annotated corpus. See **[docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md)** for the four-phase plan and **[docs/archives/decisions/DECISIONS.md](docs/archives/decisions/DECISIONS.md)** (historical ADRs) for the rationale.
8	
9	---
10	
11	## 🚀 Key Features
12	
13	*   **Pluggable Video Validation Framework**: Easily extendable base-class architecture that automatically runs checks on:
14	    *   *Static Format & Resolution*: Verifies container is MP4, resolution >= 720p, and framerate >= 30 FPS.
15	    *   *PTS Timeline Continuity*: Scans keyframe timestamps for timeline gaps, jumps, or editing splices to detect tampering or splices.
16	    *   *Scene Cut Density Check*: Measures sub-sampled HSV frame histogram correlation to verify visual cuts. A continuous raw camera stream should have near 0 cuts.
17	    *   *Blurriness Check*: Calculates average frame Laplacian variance to reject out-of-focus footage.
18	    *   *Relevance (Is it Badminton?)*: Uses high-performance HSV color court matching to identify court layouts.
19	*   **Five Pluggable Rally Segmenters** (`backend/pipeline/segmenters/` — `motion`, `gemini`, `yolo_hybrid`, `tracknet_hybrid`, `wasb_hybrid`). The three core ones:
20	    *   `yolo_hybrid` — two-stage pipeline. Stage 1 runs **torchvision person detection** (Faster R-CNN, BSD-licensed) with **ByteTrack** tracking (supervision, MIT-licensed) to cheaply filter the video down to a small set of high-motion candidate windows. Stage 2 hands each candidate (with a small padding) to the AI provider for high-precision semantic boundaries and shot counts. Massively cheaper than running the LLM on the full video. *(The registry key remains `yolo_hybrid` for back-compat; the AGPL-licensed YOLOv8 was replaced with permissive components — see [docs/archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md](docs/archives/MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md) and ADR-005.)*
21	    *   `gemini` — sends the entire video (or a `chunk_duration` slice) to Google Gemini directly. Highest precision per call, highest cost.
22	    *   `motion` — zero-dependency OpenCV pixel-difference heuristic. No API key required. Useful as a baseline or for offline/air-gapped use.
23	*   **Pluggable AI Provider Registry** (`backend/providers/`): The Gemini provider ships in-tree; new providers (e.g. an OpenAI or local-model backend) can be registered without touching the segmenters.
24	*   **Pluggable Sport Profile Registry** (`backend/sports/`): The per-sport LLM prompt and relevance config live in a `SportProfile`. Add a new sport by subclassing `SportProfile` and registering it.
25	*   **SQLite Indexing**: Each processed video is keyed by MD5 hash. Play segments (start/end/duration/server/receiver/metadata) and per-segment events are persisted in `output/sports_indexer.db`. Re-running on the same file is a cache hit.
26	*   **Query System**: Filter indexed play segments by `server`, `receiver`, `duration_min`, and `event_type` (the schema supports rich event types like smashes/serves/fouls; today the shipped segmenters mainly populate timing + estimated `shot_count`).
27	*   **Zero-Dependency Local UI**: Served statically directly by FastAPI (Vite + Node.js NOT required!) featuring ...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## `scratch/trim_video.py`

A reusable, keyframe-aware video trimmer that bakes in everything we learned this session:

```bash
python scratch/trim_video.py "in.mp4" --start 70              # lossless, instant (default)
python scratch/trim_video.py "in.mp4" --start 70 --end 240    # also trim the tail
python scratch/trim_video.py "in.mp4" --start 70 --exact      # frame-exact (re-encodes)
python scratch/trim_video.py "in.mp4" --start 70 --dry-run    # show the plan only
```

What it encodes as reusable knowledge:
- **Default = lossless keyframe-copy** — probes keyframes, snaps to the first one *at/after* `--start` so the whole unwanted region is gone (no leftover sliver), then stream-copies. Instant, zero quality loss.
- **`--exact`** re-encodes for a frame-precise cut, trying **NVENC first and falling back to CPU libx264** — so it survives the "No capable devices found" flakiness we hit.
- Reports exactly what it did (e.g. "keyframe at/after 70.0s = 70.290s; drops 0.290s more than requested, lossless").
- Defaults output to `<stem>_trimmed<ext>` beside the input; never touches the original.

It's in `scratch/` (untracked, like your other helpers). Two optional follow-ups if you want:
- **Promote it to `tools/`** as a committed, tested tool (like `cleanup_caches.py`) — say the word and I'll add it with unit tests + a README line via a PR.
- **Tidy scratch**: this session left a few one-off debug artifacts (`debug_wsl.py`, `probe_cv2_count.py`, `probe_gemini.py`, `repro_drawtext_font.py`, `repro_movie_overlay.py`) and `output/trim70.log` — want me to delete those?
